I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.

It’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.

So… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.

It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.

It started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.

It kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.

So, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.

Like at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.

Even washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.

And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`. I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. fatigue that is not relieved by rest and a marked lack of energy.\n  manifestation: Even small stuff like filling out inspection logs, driving to basketball, or washing my hair feels like it takes because I’m missing shifts and turning in inspection documentation late, and I’ve mostly stopped showing up for basketball, dance nights, and the church STEM mentoring I used to care about.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 2 years and 4 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about two years and four months, since I had to stop taking community-college classes because of money and my work schedule.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 20 / inspector_tester_sorter_sampler_or_weigher / some_college\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S024\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Like, I know you’re asking about wanting to end my life, and that feels like a really serious thing to say yes or no to, so I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound more definite than it is.\n\nI guess what I can say is… I haven’t really sorted it out in my head enough to give you a clean answer. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. If the question touches several topics, return the one it is\n  most directly about — never more than one.\n- Copy `key`, `name` and `description` exactly as they appear in [Symptom Profiles].\n- If your profile does not cover what the doctor asked, return the text \"None\" for `key`,\n  `name` and `description`, and say why in `reason`.   },

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.

Like, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. Sometimes I’ll think I hear my name or a sound at work, but usually that’s just the machines or people moving around, and when I check, there’s some normal explanation or it just kind of goes away. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.

So… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just felt really low for a long time. Like, not just a bad day or a rough week. It’s been going on for the last couple of years, and it’s kind of always there in the background.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or at church, or around people I really do care about, I still feel this heavy, empty feeling. It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. It’s not like I never smile or anything, but it feels complicated when you ask it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. It’s been pretty constant.

I’d say this has been going on for about two years and… maybe four months now. It started around when I had to stop taking community college classes, because I couldn’t really make it work with money and my work schedule. I kept thinking maybe once I adjusted, or once work settled down, I’d feel more like myself again, but it just kind of stayed there.

There might be individual days where I’m a little less down, or I can distract myself for a while, but not like a full two months where I’d say I wasn’t feeling this way. Not really.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just felt really low for a long time. Like, not just a bad day or a rough week. It’s been going on for the last couple of years, and it’s kind of always there in the background.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or at church, or around people I really do care about, I still feel this heavy, empty feeling. It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. It’s not like I never smile or anything, but it feels complicated when you ask it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. It was more gradual, but that’s the point I keep coming back to—when I had to stop school. Since then, it’s pretty much been there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. Usually I can kind of point to something, even if it’s small or probably not that big of a deal—like work stuff, or feeling like I’m behind, or worrying I said something weird. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of anxiety you mean.\n\nIt’s hard because sometimes the nervous feeling is just sort of mixed in with everything else, so I don’t always notice it as “anxiety,” exactly. I’ll just feel tense or unsettled and then later I’ll realize I’ve been worrying in the background. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything really obvious like… people telling me I’m talking too slowly, or that I’m pacing around all the time or something.

It’s been going on for the last couple of years, and it’s kind of always there in the background.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or at church, or around people I really do care about, I still feel this heavy, empty feeling. It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. It was more gradual, but that’s the point I keep coming back to—when I had to stop school. Since then, it’s pretty much been there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. Usually I can kind of point to something, even if it’s small or probably not that big of a deal—like work stuff, or feeling like I’m behind, or worrying I said something weird. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of anxiety you mean.\n\nIt’s hard because sometimes the nervous feeling is just sort of mixed in with everything else, so I don’t always notice it as “anxiety,” exactly. I’ll just feel tense or unsettled and then later I’ll realize I’ve been worrying in the background. But I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like sudden panic or being anxious all the time for no clear reason. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Like, I know you’re asking about wanting to end my life, and that feels like a really serious thing to say yes or no to, so I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound more definite than it is.\n\nI guess what I can say is… I haven’t really sorted it out in my head enough to give you a clean answer. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. Sometimes I’ll think I hear my name or a sound at work, but usually that’s just the machines or people moving around, and when I check, there’s some normal explanation or it just kind of goes away. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything really obvious like… people telling me I’m talking too slowly, or that I’m pacing around all the time or something.\n\nSometimes I feel like it takes me longer to answer people, like my brain has to kind of push through mud before I can say what I mean, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually speaking slower. And at work I’m usually trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to be doing, so I don’t really pay attention to how I’m moving unless someone points it out, which I don’t think they have.\n\nRestless… maybe sometimes I shift around or can’t get comfortable, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to sit still in a noticeable way. So I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed either one clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I can’t ever laugh at something or have one okay moment, but the things that used to actually make me feel happy or excited just don’t really do that anymore.\n\nLike basketball used to be a big thing for me. Dance nights too. And I was doing this church STEM mentoring thing that I actually cared about — like, I liked showing up and helping, and it felt like it mattered. Sometimes I just… can’t make myself go, or I think about going and it feels like too much, and also kind of pointless, even though I know I used to enjoy it.\n\nAnd it’s affecting work too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve been missing shifts and turning in my inspection documentation late. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It’s… honestly kind of with me most of the day. I don’t think it really lifts in a clean way, like where I can say, “Oh, I felt normal for the afternoon.” It’s more like it changes volume, if that makes sense.

Like sometimes if I’m at work and I’m focused on what I’m supposed to be checking or sorting, I can get through the task and not be thinking about it every second, but it’s still there underneath. Same if I’m at church or around people I care about — I might talk, I might smile a little, I might even have a moment where I’m distracted — but there’s still this heavy, empty feeling sitting there. It doesn’t really go away.

I think that’s part of why I’m here, because it’s been like that for the last couple years. Not just a bad morning or a bad night. It’s like I wake up with it, carry it around, and even if parts of the day are a little less intense, I don’t really feel free from it.

---------- turn 14 | doctor:inference | doctor.build_inference_prompt() | model=meta-llama/Llama-3.3-70B-Instruct ----------

[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n14\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF32.0, F32.1, F32.2, F32.3, F33.0, F33.1, F33.2, F33.3\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Um… I guess I’m here because I’ve just felt really low for a long time. Like, not just a bad day or a rough week. It’s been going on for the last couple of years, and it’s kind of always there in the background.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or at church, or around people I really do care about, I still feel this heavy, empty feeling. It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. It’s not like I never smile or anything, but it feels complicated when you ask it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. It was more gradual, but that’s the point I keep coming back to—when I had to stop school. Since then, it’s pretty much been there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. Usually I can kind of point to something, even if it’s small or probably not that big of a deal—like work stuff, or feeling like I’m behind, or worrying I said something weird. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of anxiety you mean.\n\nIt’s hard because sometimes the nervous feeling is just sort of mixed in with everything else, so I don’t always notice it as “anxiety,” exactly. I’ll just feel tense or unsettled and then later I’ll realize I’ve been worrying in the background. But I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like sudden panic or being anxious all the time for no clear reason. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Like, I know you’re asking about wanting to end my life, and that feels like a really serious thing to say yes or no to, so I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound more definite than it is.\n\nI guess what I can say is… I haven’t really sorted it out in my head enough to give you a clean answer. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. Sometimes I’ll think I hear my name or a sound at work, but usually that’s just the machines or people moving around, and when I check, there’s some normal explanation or it just kind of goes away. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. It’s been pretty constant.\n\nI’d say this has been going on for about two years and… maybe four months now. It started around when I had to stop taking community college classes, because I couldn’t really make it work with money and my work schedule. I kept thinking maybe once I adjusted, or once work settled down, I’d feel more like myself again, but it just kind of stayed there.\n\nThere might be individual days where I’m a little less down, or I can distract myself for a while, but not like a full two months where I’d say I wasn’t feeling this way. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything really obvious like… people telling me I’m talking too slowly, or that I’m pacing around all the time or something.\n\nSometimes I feel like it takes me longer to answer people, like my brain has to kind of push through mud before I can say what I mean, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually speaking slower. And at work I’m usually trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to be doing, so I don’t really pay attention to how I’m moving unless someone points it out, which I don’t think they have.\n\nRestless… maybe sometimes I shift around or can’t get comfortable, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to sit still in a noticeable way. So I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed either one clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I can’t ever laugh at something or have one okay moment, but the things that used to actually make me feel happy or excited just don’t really do that anymore.\n\nLike basketball used to be a big thing for me. Dance nights too. And I was doing this church STEM mentoring thing that I actually cared about — like, I liked showing up and helping, and it felt like it mattered. But I’ve mostly stopped going to all of that. It’s not even always because I’m busy. Sometimes I just… can’t make myself go, or I think about going and it feels like too much, and also kind of pointless, even though I know I used to enjoy it.\n\nAnd it’s affecting work too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve been missing shifts and turning in my inspection documentation late. I know that’s not exactly “pleasure,” but it’s like the same thing is spilling over everywhere — I’m not engaged, I’m not looking forward to anything, and even stuff I used to care about feels far away from me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood stays with you all day, or are there times during the day when you feel a little better?\n\nPatient: It’s… honestly kind of with me most of the day. I don’t think it really lifts in a clean way, like where I can say, “Oh, I felt normal for the afternoon.” It’s more like it changes volume, if that makes sense.\n\nLike sometimes if I’m at work and I’m focused on what I’m supposed to be checking or sorting, I can get through the task and not be thinking about it every second, but it’s still there underneath. Same if I’m at church or around people I care about — I might talk, I might smile a little, I might even have a moment where I’m distracted — but there’s still this heavy, empty feeling sitting there. It doesn’t really go away.\n\nI think that’s part of why I’m here, because it’s been like that for the last couple years. Not just a bad morning or a bad night. It’s like I wake up with it, carry it around, and even if parts of the day are a little less intense, I don’t really feel free from it.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }

Not like one big dramatic weight loss that I can point to, I don’t think. It’s more… my eating has been really inconsistent, and my weight kind of goes up and down.

Most days I either don’t eat much at all until my shift is almost over, because nothing sounds good or I just don’t feel like dealing with food, and then by the end I’m so drained that I’ll grab something from the vending machine — chips, candy, whatever’s easy — not because I really want it, just because I realize I haven’t eaten. So it’s not exactly a normal appetite.

I’ve noticed it mostly because my work pants fit different from month to month. Sometimes they feel looser, then later they’re tight again, and I’m like… okay, clearly something is changing, but I haven’t been tracking the number closely. So I wouldn’t say I know I lost a significant amount over two years, but there’s definitely been weight and appetite changes.

I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. It’s been pretty constant.\n\nI’d say this has been going on for about two years and… maybe four months now. It started around when I had to stop taking community college classes, because I couldn’t really make it work with money and my work schedule. And at work I’m usually trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to be doing, so I don’t really pay attention to how I’m moving unless someone points it out, which I don’t think they have.\n\nRestless… maybe sometimes I shift around or can’t get comfortable, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to sit still in a noticeable way. I don’t know that there’s one specific time where I can say, like, “it’s always worst at 6 p.m.” or something. It’s more that it’s just… there most of the time, and it kind of changes shape depending on what I’m doing.

Like mornings can be hard because I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy feeling, before anything has even happened. But then at work, even if I’m busy inspecting or sorting or trying to stay on task, it doesn’t really leave — it’s just sort of pushed to the background. And then if I’m at church or around people I care about, I can still talk and act mostly normal for a little bit, but underneath I still feel low and empty.

So I guess the best way to say it is, it’s not really tied to a time of day for me. It’s been like this for the last couple years, where I just feel low all the time, and even when I’m somewhere that should help or with people who matter to me, it doesn’t really lift.

It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. It’s not like I never smile or anything, but it feels complicated when you ask it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. It was more gradual, but that’s the point I keep coming back to—when I had to stop school. Since then, it’s pretty much been there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. Usually I can kind of point to something, even if it’s small or probably not that big of a deal—like work stuff, or feeling like I’m behind, or worrying I said something weird. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of anxiety you mean.\n\nIt’s hard because sometimes the nervous feeling is just sort of mixed in with everything else, so I don’t always notice it as “anxiety,” exactly. I’ll just feel tense or unsettled and then later I’ll realize I’ve been worrying in the background. But I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like sudden panic or being anxious all the time for no clear reason. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Like, I know you’re asking about wanting to end my life, and that feels like a really serious thing to say yes or no to, so I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound more definite than it is.\n\nI guess what I can say is… I haven’t really sorted it out in my head enough to give you a clean answer. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. Sometimes I’ll think I hear my name or a sound at work, but usually that’s just the machines or people moving around, and when I check, there’s some normal explanation or it just kind of goes away. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. It’s been pretty constant.\n\nI’d say this has been going on for about two years and… maybe four months now. It started around when I had to stop taking community college classes, because I couldn’t really make it work with money and my work schedule. I kept thinking maybe once I adjusted, or once work settled down, I’d feel more like myself again, but it just kind of stayed there.\n\nThere might be individual days where I’m a little less down, or I can distract myself for a while, but not like a full two months where I’d say I wasn’t feeling this way. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything really obvious like… people telling me I’m talking too slowly, or that I’m pacing around all the time or something.\n\nSometimes I feel like it takes me longer to answer people, like my brain has to kind of push through mud before I can say what I mean, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually speaking slower. And at work I’m usually trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to be doing, so I don’t really pay attention to how I’m moving unless someone points it out, which I don’t think they have.\n\nRestless… maybe sometimes I shift around or can’t get comfortable, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to sit still in a noticeable way. So I guess my answer is I haven’t really noticed either one clearly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to experience pleasure from activities that normally would bring you happiness?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, it’s not like I can’t ever laugh at something or have one okay moment, but the things that used to actually make me feel happy or excited just don’t really do that anymore.\n\nLike basketball used to be a big thing for me. Dance nights too. And I was doing this church STEM mentoring thing that I actually cared about — like, I liked showing up and helping, and it felt like it mattered. But I’ve mostly stopped going to all of that. It’s not even always because I’m busy. Sometimes I just… can’t make myself go, or I think about going and it feels like too much, and also kind of pointless, even though I know I used to enjoy it.\n\nAnd it’s affecting work too. I’ve gotten written warnings because I’ve been missing shifts and turning in my inspection documentation late. I know that’s not exactly “pleasure,” but it’s like the same thing is spilling over everywhere — I’m not engaged, I’m not looking forward to anything, and even stuff I used to care about feels far away from me now.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood stays with you all day, or are there times during the day when you feel a little better?\n\nPatient: It’s… honestly kind of with me most of the day. I don’t think it really lifts in a clean way, like where I can say, “Oh, I felt normal for the afternoon.” It’s more like it changes volume, if that makes sense.\n\nLike sometimes if I’m at work and I’m focused on what I’m supposed to be checking or sorting, I can get through the task and not be thinking about it every second, but it’s still there underneath. Same if I’m at church or around people I care about — I might talk, I might smile a little, I might even have a moment where I’m distracted — but there’s still this heavy, empty feeling sitting there. It doesn’t really go away.\n\nI think that’s part of why I’m here, because it’s been like that for the last couple years. Not just a bad morning or a bad night. It’s like I wake up with it, carry it around, and even if parts of the day are a little less intense, I don’t really feel free from it.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost a significant amount of weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Not like one big dramatic weight loss that I can point to, I don’t think. It’s more… my eating has been really inconsistent, and my weight kind of goes up and down.\n\nMost days I either don’t eat much at all until my shift is almost over, because nothing sounds good or I just don’t feel like dealing with food, and then by the end I’m so drained that I’ll grab something from the vending machine — chips, candy, whatever’s easy — not because I really want it, just because I realize I haven’t eaten. So it’s not exactly a normal appetite.\n\nI’ve noticed it mostly because my work pants fit different from month to month. Sometimes they feel looser, then later they’re tight again, and I’m like… okay, clearly something is changing, but I haven’t been tracking the number closely. So I wouldn’t say I know I lost a significant amount over two years, but there’s definitely been weight and appetite changes.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your low mood is worse at any particular time of day?\n\nPatient: I don’t know that there’s one specific time where I can say, like, “it’s always worst at 6 p.m.” or something. It’s more that it’s just… there most of the time, and it kind of changes shape depending on what I’m doing.\n\nLike mornings can be hard because I wake up and it’s already there, that heavy feeling, before anything has even happened. But then at work, even if I’m busy inspecting or sorting or trying to stay on task, it doesn’t really leave — it’s just sort of pushed to the background. And then if I’m at church or around people I care about, I can still talk and act mostly normal for a little bit, but underneath I still feel low and empty.\n\nSo I guess the best way to say it is, it’s not really tied to a time of day for me. It’s been like this for the last couple years, where I just feel low all the time, and even when I’m somewhere that should help or with people who matter to me, it doesn’t really lift.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Return the JSON now."
  }
]

---------- turn 17 | patient:alignment | patient.build_alignment_messages() | model=gpt-5.5 ----------
========== INPUT [patient] ==========
[
  {

I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. It’s been going on for the last couple of years, and it’s kind of always there in the background.\n\nEven when I’m at work, or at church, or around people I really do care about, I still feel this heavy, empty feeling. It doesn’t really lift. I can be sitting there with everyone and still feel like I’m carrying this weight inside me, and I don’t really know how to make it stop.\n\nDoctor: Do you still find pleasure in activities that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure how to answer that, honestly. I mean… I still do some of the same things I used to do, but I don’t always know if I’d call it “pleasure” the way I used to.\n\nLike, I can go through the motions of things, and sometimes I’ll have moments where I’m distracted or it’s okay, but it’s hard for me to tell if I’m actually enjoying it or just doing it because it’s familiar, or because I’m supposed to, or because people expect me to show up. I don’t know. It’s not like I never smile or anything, but it feels complicated when you ask it that directly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in things you used to like?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say I’ve completely lost interest… it’s more like everything feels dulled down. Like, I still know what I used to like, and sometimes I still try to do those things, but the feeling doesn’t really show up the same way.\n\nIt’s hard to explain because if someone asked me, “Do you like this?” I’d probably say yes, because in my head I know I do—or I used to. But actually wanting to do it, looking forward to it, feeling excited about it… that part feels kind of distant. Sometimes I’ll go along with something and think, maybe once I’m there it’ll feel better, but a lot of the time I’m just sort of present, not really into it.\n\nSo… yeah, maybe some interest is gone, or maybe it’s just buried under how I’ve been feeling. I’m not totally sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever thought about hurting yourself or ending your life?\n\nPatient: I… I don’t really know how to answer that cleanly. I mean, I know what you’re asking, and it’s kind of a scary question to hear out loud.\n\nI haven’t really… I don’t have a clear thing to tell you, like a specific plan or something. It’s more like sometimes when I’m feeling really weighed down, I get tired of feeling that way, and I don’t know what to do with it. But I’m not sure if that counts as what you mean.\n\nI guess I’d say I haven’t been walking around with some detailed intention to hurt myself, but the question makes me nervous because the feelings can get really heavy. I’m sorry, I’m probably not explaining it very well.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this heavy, empty feeling that you mentioned?\n\nPatient: It’s been… a little over two years, I think. About two years and four months, if I’m counting it right.\n\nIt started around when I had to stop taking my community-college classes. I couldn’t really make it work anymore because of money and my work schedule, and after that it was like this heaviness just kind of settled in. At first I thought maybe I was just disappointed or stressed and it would pass once I adjusted, but it didn’t really lift. It’s hard to separate the exact day or anything, because it wasn’t like one morning I woke up and everything was suddenly different. It was more gradual, but that’s the point I keep coming back to—when I had to stop school. Since then, it’s pretty much been there in some form.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel nervous or anxious, even when there's no apparent reason to feel that way?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I do get nervous sometimes, but I don’t know if I’d say it’s often or for no reason. Usually I can kind of point to something, even if it’s small or probably not that big of a deal—like work stuff, or feeling like I’m behind, or worrying I said something weird. But I don’t know if that’s the kind of anxiety you mean.\n\nIt’s hard because sometimes the nervous feeling is just sort of mixed in with everything else, so I don’t always notice it as “anxiety,” exactly. I’ll just feel tense or unsettled and then later I’ll realize I’ve been worrying in the background. But I wouldn’t want to say yes if you mean like sudden panic or being anxious all the time for no clear reason. I don’t think I’ve noticed it that clearly.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past two years?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I haven’t been tracking it carefully, like I’m not weighing myself every week or anything, but I’ve definitely noticed my clothes fitting differently. My work pants especially—some months they feel tighter, and then other times they’re loose enough that I notice it when I’m moving around at work.\n\nIt kind of goes along with how I’m eating, I guess. A lot of days I just don’t eat much during my shift because nothing sounds good, or I keep telling myself I’ll eat later and then suddenly the shift is almost over. And then other days, when I’m really drained, I’ll end up getting vending machine stuff late, not because I’m actually hungry in a normal way, but just because it’s there and I’m tired and I don’t have the energy to think about real food.\n\nSo, yeah, it’s been up and down. I don’t know the exact number, but enough that I’ve noticed it.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in how active or energetic you are compared to how you used to be?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. I feel like I have way less energy than I used to, and it’s not even just for big things. It’s small, normal stuff that shouldn’t feel like a whole task.\n\nLike at work, even filling out the inspection logs can feel like I’m dragging myself through it. It’s not hard exactly, I know how to do it, but it feels like it takes so much focus and energy just to sit there and get it done. And outside of work, even driving to basketball—something I used to do without thinking—can feel like this big ordeal, like I’m already tired before I even leave.\n\nEven washing my hair feels like too much sometimes. I’ll know I need to do it, and I’ll keep putting it off because standing there and going through all the steps just feels like more than I have in me. And then on my days off, I’ll think, okay, I’ll rest and then I’ll feel better, but it doesn’t really work that way. I can sleep in or lie around and still feel like I didn’t refill at all. It’s like my battery just doesn’t charge the way it used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any thoughts of wanting to end your life in the past few months?\n\nPatient: I… I’m not really sure how to answer that clearly. I don’t think I have a specific answer I can give you. Like, I know you’re asking about wanting to end my life, and that feels like a really serious thing to say yes or no to, so I don’t want to answer wrong or make it sound more definite than it is.\n\nI guess what I can say is… I haven’t really sorted it out in my head enough to give you a clean answer. Sometimes when things are bad, my thoughts get kind of heavy and everything feels like too much, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually wanting to end my life. I’m trying to think about the past few months specifically, and it’s just… hard to separate out. So I guess I’d say I’m not sure.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever heard voices or seen things that other people couldn't hear or see?\n\nPatient: Um… I don’t think so. Not in the way you mean, I guess.\n\nLike, I’ve never seen a person standing there who wasn’t actually there, or heard a voice talking to me when nobody was around. Sometimes I’ll think I hear my name or a sound at work, but usually that’s just the machines or people moving around, and when I check, there’s some normal explanation or it just kind of goes away. I don’t really have anything where I’m convinced I’m seeing or hearing something other people can’t.\n\nSo… no, I don’t think that’s been happening.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods of at least two months where you didn't feel this low?\n\nPatient: No… I don’t think I’ve had a stretch that long where I really felt okay or not low. It’s been pretty constant.\n\nI’d say this has been going on for about two years and… maybe four months now. It started around when I had to stop taking community college classes, because I couldn’t really make it work with money and my work schedule. I kept thinking maybe once I adjusted, or once work settled down, I’d feel more like myself again, but it just kind of stayed there.\n\nThere might be individual days where I’m a little less down, or I can distract myself for a while, but not like a full two months where I’d say I wasn’t feeling this way. Not really.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself moving or speaking more slowly than usual, or the opposite, feeling restless and unable to sit still?\n\nPatient: I’m not totally sure. I mean, I don’t think I’ve noticed anything really obvious like… people telling me I’m talking too slowly, or that I’m pacing around all the time or something.\n\nSometimes I feel like it takes me longer to answer people, like my brain has to kind of push through mud before I can say what I mean, but I don’t know if that’s the same as actually speaking slower. And at work I’m usually trying to keep up with what I’m supposed to be doing, so I don’t really pay attention to how I’m moving unless someone points it out, which I don’t think they have.\n\nRestless… maybe sometimes I shift around or can’t get comfortable, but I wouldn’t say I’m unable to sit still in a noticeable way.
